People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Some weeks weren’t as bad… but most of the time it’s still been there. The guilt, being worn out, not caring about things… and the death thoughts. It hasn’t really let up like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year where you felt completely back to your normal self?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Maybe a few weeks felt less heavy, but I never really felt like me again. It’s mostly just… been there most of the time. Some weeks aren’t as crushing, but the guilt and being exhausted, not caring about much, and those thoughts about death… they don’t really follow a season or anything.\n\nDoctor: Does the time of year have any effect on how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: No… I haven’t noticed the time of year changing it much.
